# Two lower points and three top-cap points are impossible

Use the C1 anchors and threshold of
[C1_EXTERIOR_REDUCTION.md](C1_EXTERIOR_REDUCTION.md):

\[
 O=(0,0),\quad H=(0,h),\quad C=(c,0),\quad
 D=(1,d),\quad F=(f,1),\qquad d\le h,\quad f\le c,
 \qquad s=93/1000.
\]

Assume every unsigned actual doubled triangle area is at least \(s\).
This note excludes the entire count

\[
 (k,j,r,t,l)=(2,0,0,3,0).
\]

Thus two free points in the actual triangle \(OHC\) cannot coexist
with three points in the top cap \(DF(1,1)\). The proof covers the
closed square, including any permitted side contacts.

## 1. Exhaustive normalization and meaning of the tables

The continuous reduction, coordinate boxes, all factor definitions,
and all inference rules are proved in
[C1_TWO_LOWER_TOP_GEOMETRY.md](C1_TWO_LOWER_TOP_GEOMETRY.md).
Choose \(Y=(u,v)\) with the smaller \(O\)-barycentric coordinate
among the two lower points. The other point \(Z=(z,w)\) is outside
\(HCY\). Transpose once, if necessary, so \(Z\in OHY\).
Order the three upper points \(P=(p,q),Q=(l,m),R=(r,t)\) by their
rays from \(O\). Their region word is TTT. These choices are
exhaustive and impose no other symmetry assumption.

The printed binary tree has one root, TTT. A line `name ABC` splits
that node into `namem` with \([ABC]<0\), and `namep` with
\([ABC]>0\). Every split determinant is formed by three actual
points. Zero would already contradict \(s>0\), so the two children
are exhaustive. Every terminal appears exactly once in the tables.

There are 202 binary refinements and 203 leaves: 183 explicit cubic
contradictions and 20 geometric contradictions. The cubics contain
45,644 positive-integer entries in total. The fixed geometric traces
have 3,644 steps, and the interval traces have 1,636 refinements.
The complete arithmetic corpus is printed below; no claim of a short
single identity replaces it.

At a leaf, replay its stated assumptions and the finite geometric
trace using Section 3 of the common note. Then replay its interval
trace from the common coarse box. The printed rational box contains
the resulting interval box. Every factor in the displayed sum is
nonnegative by Sections 4--5 of the common note. Each stated negative
upper bound is the elementary shifted-monomial bound in its Section 6.
This contradicts nonnegativity of that leaf's entire sum.

The 20 geometric leaves consist of twelve interval contradictions,
seven clustered interior triples, and one convex five-point top hull.
The common note supplies the corresponding universal exclusions and
explains the endpoint support normalization of the final hull case.
All their actual witnesses and edge signs are printed.

The companion [check_c1_two_lower_top.py](check_c1_two_lower_top.py),
invoked with `TTT`, reads this manuscript, checks the exhaustive tree,
replays every geometric and interval step, reconstructs every factor
from its definition, and verifies all 183 sums with exact rational
arithmetic. It imports no discovery data and calls no optimizer. The
mathematical proof is the continuous reduction and these finite
printed identities.
